</p><p>In February 2020 <a href="/wiki/2020_British_cabinet_reshuffle" title="2020 British cabinet reshuffle">a reshuffle</a> took place following <a href="/wiki/2019_United_Kingdom_general_election" title="2019 United Kingdom general election">the general election</a> which had been held in December.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECollins2020_153-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECollins2020-153">&#91;149&#93;</a></sup> Truss feared that she would be dismissed after the blunders she had made on her previous international trips, but Johnson decided to keep her in post following <a href="/wiki/Sajid_Javid" title="Sajid Javid">Sajid Javid</a>'s resignation as chancellor.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEColeHeale2022193_154-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEColeHeale2022193-154">&#91;150&#93;</a></sup> During her time at the department, Truss became notorious for leaking information, with <a href="/wiki/Dominic_Cummings" title="Dominic Cummings">Dominic Cummings</a>, Johnson's chief adviser, saying that Truss was "the only minister I shouted at in Number 10" because of her "compulsive pathological leaking".<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEColeHeale2022197_155-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEColeHeale2022197-155">&#91;151&#93;</a></sup> Truss's pursuit of a trade deal with the US concerned the <a href="/wiki/National_Farmers%27_Union_of_England_and_Wales" title="National Farmers&#39; Union of England and Wales">National Farmers' Union</a> (NFU), which worried about an influx of lower-quality food products if passed; the NFU, along with <i>The Mail on Sunday</i>, campaigned against such a deal that spring.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEColeHeale2022195_and_204_156-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEColeHeale2022195_and_204-156">&#91;152&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/COVID-19_lockdown_in_the_United_Kingdom" title="COVID-19 lockdown in the United Kingdom">COVID-19 lockdowns</a> eliminated international travel, with Truss attending virtual meetings; as a result, she became closer to her family.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEColeHeale2022198_157-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEColeHeale2022198-157">&#91;153&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>By <a href="/wiki/Presidential_transition_of_Joe_Biden" title="Presidential transition of Joe Biden">early 2021</a>, Truss's attempted US trade deal was accepted as futile.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEColeHeale2022200_158-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEColeHeale2022200-158">&#91;154&#93;</a></sup> Instead, she had focussed on <a href="/wiki/Accession_of_the_United_Kingdom_to_CPTPP" title="Accession of the United Kingdom to CPTPP">joining</a> the <a href="/wiki/CPTPP" class="mw-redirect" title="CPTPP">CPTPP</a>, which necessitated <a href="/wiki/Free_trade_agreements_of_the_United_Kingdom" title="Free trade agreements of the United Kingdom">free trade agreements</a> with Australia,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEYorke2020_159-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEYorke2020-159">&#91;155&#93;</a></sup> Japan,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEParker2020_160-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEParker2020-160">&#91;156&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTE&quot;UK_signs_first_major_post-Brexit_trade_deal_with_Japan&quot;._BBC_News_161-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTE&quot;UK_signs_first_major_post-Brexit_trade_deal_with_Japan&quot;._BBC_News-161">&#91;157&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEBurden2020_162-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBurden2020-162">&#91;158&#93;</a></sup> and New Zealand.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMcGuiness2021_163-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMcGuiness2021-163">&#91;159&#93;</a></sup> The Australia deal, finalised in December was described as "the hardest thing she's ever got through"; the New Zealand deal was agreed shortly thereafter.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEColeHeale2022204_164-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEColeHeale2022204-164">&#91;160&#93;</a></sup> By mid-2021, she had started to ingratiate herself with the parliamentary party in the event of a leadership election.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEColeHeale2022209_165-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEColeHeale2022209-165">&#91;161&#93;</a></sup> In September, plans for a <a href="/wiki/National_Insurance" title="National Insurance">National Insurance</a> increase were opposed by Truss; Downing Street expected her resignation, but Truss later privately decided against it.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEColeHeale2022209,_211–213_166-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEColeHeale2022209,_211–213-166">&#91;162&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span id="Foreign_secretary_.282021.E2.80.932022.29"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="Foreign_secretary_(2021–2022)">Foreign secretary (2021–2022)</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Liz_Truss&amp;action=edit&amp;section=11" title="Edit section: Foreign secretary (2021–2022)">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Foreign_Secretary_Liz_Truss_visits_Moscow_Russia_(51875320408).jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img alt="Truss, amongst others, walking in Red Square in Moscow" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/53/Foreign_Secretary_Liz_Truss_visits_Moscow_Russia_%2851875320408%29.jpg/280px-Foreign_Secretary_Liz_Truss_visits_Moscow_Russia_%2851875320408%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="280" height="198" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/53/Foreign_Secretary_Liz_Truss_visits_Moscow_Russia_%2851875320408%29.jpg/420px-Foreign_Secretary_Liz_Truss_visits_Moscow_Russia_%2851875320408%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/53/Foreign_Secretary_Liz_Truss_visits_Moscow_Russia_%2851875320408%29.jpg/560px-Foreign_Secretary_Liz_Truss_visits_Moscow_Russia_%2851875320408%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="4474" data-file-height="3160" /></a><figcaption>Truss visiting <a href="/wiki/Red_Square" title="Red Square">Red Square</a> the day prior to her 2022 meeting with Lavrov</figcaption></figure><p>In September 2021, during a <a href="/wiki/2021_British_cabinet_reshuffle" title="2021 British cabinet reshuffle">cabinet reshuffle</a>, Johnson promoted Truss from international trade secretary to secretary of state for foreign, Commonwealth and development affairs, replacing <a href="/wiki/Dominic_Raab" title="Dominic Raab">Dominic Raab</a>, who had been criticised for holidaying in <a href="/wiki/Crete" title="Crete">Crete</a> during the <a href="/wiki/Fall_of_Kabul_(2021)" title="Fall of Kabul (2021)">Fall of Kabul</a>;<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEPayne20229ColeHeale2022215_and_217_167-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEPayne20229ColeHeale2022215_and_217-167">&#91;163&#93;</a></sup> Truss became the second woman to occupy the office and kept the post of equalities minister.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEColeHeale2022217_168-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEColeHeale2022217-168">&#91;164&#93;</a></sup> Her early actions as foreign secretary included negotiating the release of <a href="/wiki/Nazanin_Zaghari-Ratcliffe" title="Nazanin Zaghari-Ratcliffe">Nazanin Zaghari-Ratcliffe</a> at the <a href="/wiki/United_Nations_General_Assembly" title="United Nations General Assembly">UN General Assembly</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECheeseman2021_169-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECheeseman2021-169">&#91;165&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-171" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-171">&#91;e&#93;</a></sup> meeting with her Japanese, Canadian and German counterparts, mounting an unsuccessful attempt to join the <a href="/wiki/United_States%E2%80%93Mexico%E2%80%93Canada_Agreement" title="United States–Mexico–Canada Agreement">United States–Mexico–Canada Agreement</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEColeHeale2022219_172-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEColeHeale2022219-172">&#91;167&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEBlewett2021_173-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBlewett2021-173">&#91;168&#93;</a></sup> and a visit to Estonia where, like <a href="/wiki/Margaret_Thatcher" title="Margaret Thatcher">Margaret Thatcher</a>, she was photographed in a tank,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTESwinford2021_174-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTESwinford2021-174">&#91;169&#93;</a></sup> generating both praise and mockery.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEColeHeale2022220_175-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEColeHeale2022220-175">&#91;170&#93;</a></sup>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1062260506"></p><div class="quotebox pullquote floatleft" style="width:200px; ; background-color: #E0E0F8;">
<blockquote class="quotebox-quote left-aligned" style="">
<p>[W]ho was the person to get the job? Someone who'd been in international trade and travelled around the world for two-and-a-half years. That was a natural promotion.
</p>
</blockquote>
<p><cite class="right-aligned" style=""><a href="/wiki/Kwasi_Kwarteng" title="Kwasi Kwarteng">Kwasi Kwarteng</a> on Truss's promotion to foreign secretary.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEColeHeale2022217_168-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEColeHeale2022217-168">&#91;164&#93;</a></sup></cite>
</p>
</div>
<p>In early 2022 Truss became preoccupied with a <a href="/wiki/Prelude_to_the_Russian_invasion_of_Ukraine" title="Prelude to the Russian invasion of Ukraine">build-up of Russian troops</a> near the <a href="/wiki/Russia%E2%80%93Ukraine_border" title="Russia–Ukraine border">Russia–Ukraine border</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEColeHeale2022219_172-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEColeHeale2022219-172">&#91;167&#93;</a></sup> Truss supported a plan which <a href="/wiki/Declassification#United_Kingdom" title="Declassification">declassified</a> a large amount of intelligence on Russia, releasing it to the public for the first time to "catch the <a href="/wiki/Kremlin" title="Kremlin">Kremlin</a> off guard" in the event of an invasion.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEColeHeale2022219_172-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEColeHeale2022219-172">&#91;167&#93;</a></sup> On 10 February 2022, she met the Russian foreign minister <a href="/wiki/Sergey_Lavrov" title="Sergey Lavrov">Sergey Lavrov</a> in Moscow, becoming the first minister to do so since the <a href="/wiki/Poisoning_of_Sergei_and_Yulia_Skripal" title="Poisoning of Sergei and Yulia Skripal">2018 Salisbury poisonings</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEPayne202283–84ColeHeale2022229_176-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEPayne202283–84ColeHeale2022229-176">&#91;171&#93;</a></sup> The meeting was reportedly difficult; Lavrov described communicating with Truss as "like talking to a deaf person".<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEPhilpParfittWaterfield2022_177-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEPhilpParfittWaterfield2022-177">&#91;172&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEPayne202284_178-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEPayne202284-178">&#91;173&#93;</a></sup> Five days later, Truss stated that the world was on the "brink of war in Europe";<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEGrierson2022b_179-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEGrierson2022b-179">&#91;174&#93;</a></sup> this transpired in the early hours of 24 February as <a href="/wiki/Russian_invasion_of_Ukraine" title="Russian invasion of Ukraine">Russia invaded Ukraine</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEPayne202271_180-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEPayne202271-180">&#91;175&#93;</a></sup> Before and during the immediate aftermath of the invasion, Truss strongly advocated <a href="/wiki/International_sanctions_during_the_Russian_invasion_of_Ukraine" title="International sanctions during the Russian invasion of Ukraine">sanctions on Russia</a>, encouraging other <a href="/wiki/G7" title="G7">G7</a> leaders to impose them;<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEColeHeale2022220_175-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEColeHeale2022220-175">&#91;170&#93;</a></sup> in March 2022, she stated that they would end only in the event of a "full ceasefire and withdrawal".<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTE&quot;Ukraine_war:_Liz_Truss_says_Russia_sanctions_should_end_only_after_withdrawal&quot;._BBC_News_181-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTE&quot;Ukraine_war:_Liz_Truss_says_Russia_sanctions_should_end_only_after_withdrawal&quot;._BBC_News-181">&#91;176&#93;</a></sup> Johnson praised Truss's actions, saying that "she was always terrific on Ukraine<span class="nowrap">&#160;</span>... other governments <a href="https://en.wiktionary.org/wiki/faffed" class="extiw" title="wikt:faffed">faffed</a> around<span class="nowrap">&#160;</span>... she was very clear and focused".<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEColeHeale2022231–232_182-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEColeHeale2022231–232-182">&#91;177&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Throughout the first half of 2022, Johnson's position as prime minister became increasingly unstable owing to successive scandals <a href="/wiki/July_2022_United_Kingdom_government_crisis" title="July 2022 United Kingdom government crisis">damaging his government</a> and his personal reputation,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEPayne202211HicksonWilliams20233_183-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEPayne202211HicksonWilliams20233-183">&#91;178&#93;</a></sup> including <a href="/wiki/Partygate" title="Partygate">Partygate</a>, which resulted in him and the chancellor <a href="/wiki/Rishi_Sunak" title="Rishi Sunak">Rishi Sunak</a> receiving <a href="/wiki/Fixed_penalty_notice" title="Fixed penalty notice">fixed penalty notices</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEPayne202297_184-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEPayne202297-184">&#91;179&#93;</a></sup> During this time, Truss announced the <a href="/wiki/Northern_Ireland_Protocol_Bill" title="Northern Ireland Protocol Bill">Northern Ireland Protocol Bill</a>, which was intended to overhaul the <a href="/wiki/Northern_Ireland_Protocol" title="Northern Ireland Protocol">Northern Ireland Protocol</a>, including measures to liberate goods produced in Great Britain from the "unnecessary bureaucracy" entering Northern Ireland;<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEColeHeale2022233_185-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEColeHeale2022233-185">&#91;180&#93;</a></sup> the plan was criticised by the <a href="/wiki/European_Commission" title="European Commission">European Commission</a> but was received well by the <a href="/wiki/European_Research_Group" title="European Research Group">European Research Group</a> and the <a href="/wiki/Democratic_Unionist_Party" title="Democratic Unionist Party">Democratic Unionist Party</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMorris2022b_186-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMorris2022b-186">&#91;181&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEColeHeale2022233_185-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEColeHeale2022233-185">&#91;180&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEElgotO&#39;Carroll2022_187-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEElgotO&#39;Carroll2022-187">&#91;182&#93;</a></sup> Amid mounting pressure on Johnson in lieu of the <a href="/wiki/Chris_Pincher_scandal" title="Chris Pincher scandal">Chris Pincher scandal</a>, on 5 July Sunak and Sajid Javid resigned within minutes of each other, with Johnson again considering giving Truss the chancellorship; he decided against it and instead selected <a href="/wiki/Nadhim_Zahawi" title="Nadhim Zahawi">Nadhim Zahawi</a> as Sunak's replacement.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEPayne2022183–185_188-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEPayne2022183–185-188">&#91;183&#93;</a></sup> Johnson's premiership proved untenable, however, and on 7 July, he announced his resignation as leader of the Conservative Party, a move which Truss called "the right decision".<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEPayne2022226_and_229_189-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEPayne2022226_and_229-189">&#91;184&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span id="Leadership_election_.28July.E2.80.93September_2022.29"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="Leadership_election_(July–September_2022)">Leadership election (July–September 2022)</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Liz_Truss&amp;action=edit&amp;section=12" title="Edit section: Leadership election (July–September 2022)">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1033289096">.mw-parser-output .hatnote{font-style:italic}.mw-parser-output div.hatnote{padding-left:1.6em;margin-bottom:0.5em}.mw-parser-output .hatnote i{font-style:normal}.mw-parser-output .hatnote+link+.hatnote{margin-top:-0.5em}</style><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/July%E2%80%93September_2022_Conservative_Party_leadership_election" title="July–September 2022 Conservative Party leadership election">July–September 2022 Conservative Party leadership election</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Liz_For_Leader_logo.png" class="mw-file-description"><img alt="&quot;Liz for Leader&quot; written in blue block capitals." src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/02/Liz_For_Leader_logo.png/260px-Liz_For_Leader_logo.png" decoding="async" width="260" height="100" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/02/Liz_For_Leader_logo.png/390px-Liz_For_Leader_logo.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/02/Liz_For_Leader_logo.png/520px-Liz_For_Leader_logo.png 2x" data-file-width="3375" data-file-height="1294" /></a><figcaption>Logo for Truss's leadership bid</figcaption></figure>
<p>On 10 July Truss announced her intention to run in the <a href="/wiki/July%E2%80%93September_2022_Conservative_Party_leadership_election" title="July–September 2022 Conservative Party leadership election">leadership election</a> to replace Johnson. She pledged to cut taxes if elected and said she would "fight the election as a Conservative and govern as a Conservative" and take "immediate action to help people deal with the cost of living".<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEWalker2022_190-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWalker2022-190">&#91;185&#93;</a></sup> She said she would cancel a planned rise in <a href="/wiki/United_Kingdom_corporation_tax" title="United Kingdom corporation tax">corporation tax</a> and reverse the increase in National Insurance rates, funded by delaying the date by which the national debt was planned to fall, as part of a "long-term plan to bring down the size of the state and the tax burden".<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTERiley-Smith2022_191-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTERiley-Smith2022-191">&#91;186&#93;</a></sup> The political scientist <a href="/wiki/Vernon_Bogdanor" title="Vernon Bogdanor">Vernon Bogdanor</a> said in a 2022 article that "she appreciated that winning over the membership required not detailed policy proposals but the creation of a mood".<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEBogdanor2022569_192-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBogdanor2022569-192">&#91;187&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Truss received 50 votes on the first of Conservative MPs' <a href="/wiki/July%E2%80%93September_2022_Conservative_Party_leadership_election#Results" title="July–September 2022 Conservative Party leadership election">five ballots</a>, with the number of votes cast for her increasing in each;<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEBogdanor2022566_193-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBogdanor2022566-193">&#91;188&#93;</a></sup> on 20 July Truss and Sunak were chosen by the parliamentary party to be put forward to the membership for the final leadership vote, with Truss receiving 113 votes to Sunak's 137.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMiddleton202313_194-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMiddleton202313-194">&#91;189&#93;</a></sup> In the membership vote, the leader of the <a href="/wiki/1922_Committee" title="1922 Committee">1922 Committee</a>, <a href="/wiki/Graham_Brady" title="Graham Brady">Graham Brady</a>, announced on 5 September that 43 per cent of ballots were for Sunak and 57 per cent for Truss, making her the new leader.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEPayne2022250_195-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEPayne2022250-195">&#91;190&#93;</a></sup> In Truss's victory speech, she said that she would deliver on her campaign promises and pledged to win a "great victory" for the Conservatives at the next general election.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEPayne2022250_195-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEPayne2022250-195">&#91;190&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span id="Premiership_.28September.E2.80.93October_2022.29"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="Premiership_(September–October_2022)">Premiership (September–October 2022)</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Liz_Truss&amp;action=edit&amp;section=13" title="Edit section: Premiership (September–October 2022)">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Premiership_of_Liz_Truss" title="Premiership of Liz Truss">Premiership of Liz Truss</a></div>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1096954695/mw-parser-output/.tmulti">.mw-parser-output .tmulti .multiimageinner{display:flex;flex-direction:column}.mw-parser-output .tmulti //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/ee/PM_Liz_Truss_announces_her_resignation_%28cropped%29.jpg/600px-PM_Liz_Truss_announces_her_resignation_%28cropped%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1136" <h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Cabinet_and_death_of_Elizabeth_II">Cabinet and death of Elizabeth II</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Liz_Truss&amp;action=edit&amp;section=14" title="Edit section: Cabinet and death of Elizabeth II">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main articles: <a href="/wiki/Truss_ministry" title="Truss ministry">Truss ministry</a> and <a href="/wiki/Death_and_state_funeral_of_Elizabeth_II" title="Death and state funeral of Elizabeth II">Death and state funeral of Elizabeth II</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Prime_Minister_Liz_Truss_chairing_the_first_meeting_of_her_Cabinet.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img alt="A room full of people sitting in a white room at a dark green table. Truss is roughly in the centre-left of the image, smiling." src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/1c/Prime_Minister_Liz_Truss_chairing_the_first_meeting_of_her_Cabinet.jpg/260px-Prime_Minister_Liz_Truss_chairing_the_first_meeting_of_her_Cabinet.jpg" decoding="async" width="260" height="174" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/1c/Prime_Minister_Liz_Truss_chairing_the_first_meeting_of_her_Cabinet.jpg/390px-Prime_Minister_Liz_Truss_chairing_the_first_meeting_of_her_Cabinet.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/1c/Prime_Minister_Liz_Truss_chairing_the_first_meeting_of_her_Cabinet.jpg/520px-Prime_Minister_Liz_Truss_chairing_the_first_meeting_of_her_Cabinet.jpg 2x" data-file-width="4096" data-file-height="2743" /></a><figcaption>Truss chairing the first meeting of her cabinet</figcaption></figure>
<p>As the leader of the Conservative Party, the majority party in the House of Commons, Truss was appointed as prime minister by <a href="/wiki/Elizabeth_II" title="Elizabeth II">Elizabeth II</a> at <a href="/wiki/Balmoral_Castle" title="Balmoral Castle">Balmoral Castle</a> on 6 September 2022 and began to select her cabinet ministers.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMiddleton20239_196-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMiddleton20239-196">&#91;191&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEAllen202336_197-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEAllen202336-197">&#91;192&#93;</a></sup> With the appointment of Kwarteng as chancellor of the Exchequer, <a href="/wiki/James_Cleverly" title="James Cleverly">James Cleverly</a> as foreign secretary, and <a href="/wiki/Suella_Braverman" title="Suella Braverman">Suella Braverman</a> as home secretary, for the first time in British history, no white men held any of the <a href="/wiki/Great_Offices_of_State" title="Great Offices of State">Great Offices of State</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMiddleton20239_196-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMiddleton20239-196">&#91;191&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEZeffman2022a_198-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEZeffman2022a-198">&#91;193&#93;</a></sup> Other appointments included Thérèse Coffey as deputy prime minister and health secretary, <a href="/wiki/Jacob_Rees-Mogg" title="Jacob Rees-Mogg">Jacob Rees-Mogg</a> as business secretary, <a href="/wiki/Kemi_Badenoch" title="Kemi Badenoch">Kemi Badenoch</a> as international trade secretary, <a href="/wiki/Kit_Malthouse" title="Kit Malthouse">Kit Malthouse</a> as education secretary, <a href="/wiki/Penny_Mordaunt" title="Penny Mordaunt">Penny Mordaunt</a> as <a href="/wiki/Leader_of_the_House_of_Commons" title="Leader of the House of Commons">leader of the House of Commons</a> and <a href="/wiki/Michelle_Donelan" title="Michelle Donelan">Michelle Donelan</a> as <a href="/wiki/Secretary_of_State_for_Culture,_Media_and_Sport" title="Secretary of State for Culture, Media and Sport">culture secretary</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMiddleton20235–6_199-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMiddleton20235–6-199">&#91;194&#93;</a></sup> Truss retained <a href="/wiki/Ben_Wallace_(politician)" title="Ben Wallace (politician)">Ben Wallace</a> as defence secretary, <a href="/wiki/Alok_Sharma" title="Alok Sharma">Alok Sharma</a> as <a href="/wiki/President_for_COP26" class="mw-redirect" title="President for COP26">president for COP26</a>, <a href="/wiki/Alister_Jack" title="Alister Jack">Alister Jack</a> as <a href="/wiki/Secretary_of_State_for_Scotland" title="Secretary of State for Scotland">Scottish secretary</a>, <a href="/wiki/Robert_Buckland" title="Robert Buckland">Robert Buckland</a> as <a href="/wiki/Secretary_of_State_for_Wales" title="Secretary of State for Wales">Wales secretary</a>, and <a href="/wiki/James_Heappey" title="James Heappey">James Heappey</a> as <a href="/wiki/Minister_of_State_for_the_Armed_Forces" title="Minister of State for the Armed Forces">minister of state for the armed forces and veterans</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEPayneCameron-ChileshePickard2022_200-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEPayneCameron-ChileshePickard2022-200">&#91;195&#93;</a></sup> Her cabinet was notably composed almost entirely of those who had supported her during the leadership contest.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEZeffman2022b_201-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEZeffman2022b-201">&#91;196&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Truss was the fifteenth and final British <a href="/wiki/List_of_prime_ministers_of_Elizabeth_II" title="List of prime ministers of Elizabeth II">prime minister to serve under Elizabeth II</a>, who died on 8 September, two days after appointing Truss.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMarsh2023122_202-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMarsh2023122-202">&#91;197&#93;</a></sup> She was told of the Queen's ill health in the early morning and that it was a "matter of hours, not days";<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEWoodcock2022b_203-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWoodcock2022b-203">&#91;198&#93;</a></sup> Truss ordered black clothes from her Greenwich home in the anticipation of the Queen's death, as she had not had time to move her belongings to Westminster.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEColeHeale2022281_204-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEColeHeale2022281-204">&#91;199&#93;</a></sup> Upon Elizabeth's death, Truss delivered a statement outside 10 Downing Street paying tribute to her:
</p>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r996844942"><blockquote class="templatequote"><p>Queen Elizabeth II was the rock on which modern Britain was built. Our country has grown and flourished under her reign. Britain is the great country it is today because of her<span class="nowrap">&#160;</span>... Through thick and thin, Queen Elizabeth II provided us with the stability and the strength that we needed. She was the very spirit of Great Britain, and that spirit will endure.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTE&quot;In_full:_Prime_Minister_Liz_Truss_pays_tribute_to_Queen&quot;._BBC_News_205-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTE&quot;In_full:_Prime_Minister_Liz_Truss_pays_tribute_to_Queen&quot;._BBC_News-205">&#91;200&#93;</a></sup></p></blockquote>
<p>On 10 September she attended <a href="/wiki/Proclamation_of_accession_of_Charles_III" title="Proclamation of accession of Charles III">Charles III's accession ceremony</a> and took an oath of allegiance to the King with fellow senior MPs.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTERatcliffe2022_206-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTERatcliffe2022-206">&#91;201&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTE&quot;Senior_MPs_take_oath_of_allegiance_to_King_Charles&quot;._BBC_News_207-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTE&quot;Senior_MPs_take_oath_of_allegiance_to_King_Charles&quot;._BBC_News-207">&#91;202&#93;</a></sup> On 19 September she attended <a href="/wiki/Death_and_state_funeral_of_Elizabeth_II" title="Death and state funeral of Elizabeth II">the Queen's funeraI service</a> in <a href="/wiki/Westminster_Abbey" title="Westminster Abbey">Westminster Abbey</a>, reading the second <a href="/wiki/Lection" title="Lection">lesson</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTE&quot;Queen_Elizabeth&#39;s_funeral:_Order_of_service_at_Westminster_Abbey&quot;._BBC_News_208-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTE&quot;Queen_Elizabeth&#39;s_funeral:_Order_of_service_at_Westminster_Abbey&quot;._BBC_News-208">&#91;203&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Domestic_policies_and_mini-budget">Domestic policies and mini-budget</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Liz_Truss&amp;action=edit&amp;section=15" title="Edit section: Domestic policies and mini-budget">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/September_2022_United_Kingdom_mini-budget" title="September 2022 United Kingdom mini-budget">September 2022 United Kingdom mini-budget</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:President_Joe_Biden_meets_with_United_Kingdom_Prime_Minister_Liz_Truss.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img alt="Truss and Biden sit on chairs with a blue fabric draped behind them, with US and UK flags in the background. There is a table between them and Truss appears to be saying something." src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/97/President_Joe_Biden_meets_with_United_Kingdom_Prime_Minister_Liz_Truss.jpg/260px-President_Joe_Biden_meets_with_United_Kingdom_Prime_Minister_Liz_Truss.jpg" decoding="async" width="260" height="173" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/97/President_Joe_Biden_meets_with_United_Kingdom_Prime_Minister_Liz_Truss.jpg/390px-President_Joe_Biden_meets_with_United_Kingdom_Prime_Minister_Liz_Truss.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/97/President_Joe_Biden_meets_with_United_Kingdom_Prime_Minister_Liz_Truss.jpg/520px-President_Joe_Biden_meets_with_United_Kingdom_Prime_Minister_Liz_Truss.jpg 2x" data-file-width="4096" data-file-height="2730" /></a><figcaption>Truss with US president <a href="/wiki/Joe_Biden" title="Joe Biden">Joe Biden</a> in September 2022</figcaption></figure><p>On 8 September, in response to the <a href="/wiki/2021%E2%80%93present_United_Kingdom_cost-of-living_crisis" title="2021–present United Kingdom cost-of-living crisis">ongoing cost of living crisis</a>, Truss announced the <a href="/wiki/Energy_Price_Guarantee" class="mw-redirect" title="Energy Price Guarantee">Energy Price Guarantee</a>, which was planned to cap average household energy bills at £2,500 per year,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEThomas2022a_209-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEThomas2022a-209">&#91;204&#93;</a></sup> costing between 31 and £140 billion for the two years it covered.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTELawlessHui2022_210-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTELawlessHui2022-210">&#91;205&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTESillars2022_211-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTESillars2022-211">&#91;206&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEWorthy2022719_212-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWorthy2022719-212">&#91;207&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTE&quot;The_Growth_Plan_2022&quot;._gov.uk25_213-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTE&quot;The_Growth_Plan_2022&quot;._gov.uk25-213">&#91;208&#93;</a></sup> Truss, who announced the measure in the House of Commons, made an effort to keep the energy cap and the tax plan announcements—which the chancellor was planned to unveil—separate.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEColeHeale2022277_214-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEColeHeale2022277-214">&#91;209&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>On 23 September, Kwarteng announced a <a href="/wiki/September_2022_United_Kingdom_mini-budget" title="September 2022 United Kingdom mini-budget">controversial mini-budget</a> which proposed cutting taxation significantly, including abolishing the 45 per cent <a href="/wiki/Tax_rate" title="Tax rate">rate of tax</a> and the proposed <a href="/wiki/Health_and_Social_Care_Levy" title="Health and Social Care Levy">Health and Social Care Levy</a>, cutting <a href="/wiki/Stamp_duty_in_the_United_Kingdom" title="Stamp duty in the United Kingdom">stamp duty</a> and the basic rate of <a href="/wiki/Income_tax" title="Income tax">income tax</a> and cancelling rises in National Insurance contributions and <a href="/wiki/Corporate_tax" title="Corporate tax">corporation tax</a>;<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTE&quot;At_a_glance:_What&#39;s_in_the_mini-budget?&quot;._BBC_News_215-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTE&quot;At_a_glance:_What&#39;s_in_the_mini-budget?&quot;._BBC_News-215">&#91;210&#93;</a></sup> the package, which had been constructed by Truss and Kwarteng together,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMarsh2023121_216-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMarsh2023121-216">&#91;211&#93;</a></sup> was to be funded by borrowing and was intended to stimulate <a href="/wiki/Economic_growth" title="Economic growth">growth</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMarsh2023122_202-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMarsh2023122-202">&#91;197&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDiamondRichardsSandersWestwood20237_217-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDiamondRichardsSandersWestwood20237-217">&#91;212&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHicksonWilliams20238_218-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHicksonWilliams20238-218">&#91;213&#93;</a></sup> The mini-budget was received badly by financial markets because of its inclusion of temporary spending measures while tax rates were permanently cut,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEPulligLeón-Ledesma2022_219-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEPulligLeón-Ledesma2022-219">&#91;214&#93;</a></sup> was blamed for the pound falling to its lowest ever rate against the <a href="/wiki/United_States_dollar" title="United States dollar">US dollar</a>, (US$1.033)<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTE&quot;Pound_hits_record_low_after_tax_cut_plans&quot;._BBC_News_220-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTE&quot;Pound_hits_record_low_after_tax_cut_plans&quot;._BBC_News-220">&#91;215&#93;</a></sup> and prompted <a href="/wiki/September_2022_United_Kingdom_mini-budget#Currency,_interest_rate,_and_debt" title="September 2022 United Kingdom mini-budget">a response</a> from the <a href="/wiki/Bank_of_England" title="Bank of England">Bank of England</a>; the public reaction was also broadly negative.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECohen2022_221-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECohen2022-221">&#91;216&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEInman2022_222-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEInman2022-222">&#91;217&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTESmith2022_223-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTESmith2022-223">&#91;218&#93;</a></sup> The mini-budget was criticised by the <a href="/wiki/International_Monetary_Fund" title="International Monetary Fund">IMF</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMarsh2023122–123_224-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMarsh2023122–123-224">&#91;219&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHickey2022_225-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHickey2022-225">&#91;220&#93;</a></sup> the US president <a href="/wiki/Joe_Biden" title="Joe Biden">Joe Biden</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECrerar2022_226-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECrerar2022-226">&#91;221&#93;</a></sup> the Labour Party and many within Truss's party, including senior politicians Michael Gove and <a href="/wiki/Grant_Shapps" title="Grant Shapps">Grant Shapps</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEWorthy2022721–722_227-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWorthy2022721–722-227">&#91;222&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEPayne2022267_228-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEPayne2022267-228">&#91;223&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Government_crisis_and_resignation">Government crisis and resignation</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Liz_Truss&amp;action=edit&amp;section=16" title="Edit section: Government crisis and resignation">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/October_2022_United_Kingdom_government_crisis" title="October 2022 United Kingdom government crisis">October 2022 United Kingdom government crisis</a></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1062260506"><div class="quotebox pullquote floatright" style="width:250px; ; background-color: #E0E0F8;">
<blockquote class="quotebox-quote left-aligned" style="">
<p>I think it's a <a href="https://en.wiktionary.org/wiki/shambles#Noun" class="extiw" title="wikt:shambles">shambles</a> and a disgrace<span class="nowrap">&#160;</span>... I hope all those people that put Liz Truss into Number 10, I hope it was worth it<span class="nowrap">&#160;</span>... because the damage they have done to our party is extraordinary.
</p>
</blockquote>
<p><cite class="left-aligned" style=""><a href="/wiki/Charles_Walker_(British_politician)" title="Charles Walker (British politician)">Charles Walker</a>'s remarks on the market fallout, subsequent government crisis and Conservative unpopularity.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEColeHeale2022308_229-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEColeHeale2022308-229">&#91;224&#93;</a></sup></cite>
</p>
</div>
<p>After initially defending the mini-budget, on 3 October Truss instructed Kwarteng to reverse the abolition of the 45 per cent income tax additional rate.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEEardley2022_230-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEEardley2022-230">&#91;225&#93;</a></sup> She later reversed the cut in corporation tax and dismissed Kwarteng, replacing him with <a href="/wiki/Jeremy_Hunt" title="Jeremy Hunt">Jeremy Hunt</a> on 14 October.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEPayne2022267_228-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEPayne2022267-228">&#91;223&#93;</a></sup> Hunt reversed many of the remaining policies announced in the mini-budget, leading to further instability.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEBogdanor2022570_231-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBogdanor2022570-231">&#91;226&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKutllovci2022223–224_232-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKutllovci2022223–224-232">&#91;227&#93;</a></sup> Because of Truss's perceived weakness, he was described as the <span title="Latin-language text"><i lang="la"><a href="/wiki/De_facto" title="De facto">de facto</a></i></span> prime minister.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDavies2022_233-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDavies2022-233">&#91;228&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEO&#39;Grady2022_234-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEO&#39;Grady2022-234">&#91;229&#93;</a></sup> During this time, Truss became increasingly unpopular with the public, and contributed to a large fall in support for the Conservatives;<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEWorthy2022720_and_722_235-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWorthy2022720_and_722-235">&#91;230&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTELi20233_236-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTELi20233-236">&#91;231&#93;</a></sup> in October, she became the most unpopular prime minister in British history,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMiddleton202313_194-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMiddleton202313-194">&#91;189&#93;</a></sup> with her personal approval rating recorded as nine per cent.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTETurner2022_237-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTETurner2022-237">&#91;232&#93;</a></sup> She was pilloried in national and international press as a <a href="/wiki/Flip-flop_(politics)" title="Flip-flop (politics)">u-turner</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTE&quot;Newspaper_headlines:_Liz_Truss_&#39;faces_new_Tory_rebellion&#39;_after_tax_cut_U-turn&quot;._BBC_News_238-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTE&quot;Newspaper_headlines:_Liz_Truss_&#39;faces_new_Tory_rebellion&#39;_after_tax_cut_U-turn&quot;._BBC_News-238">&#91;233&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEWalkerElgotAllegretti2022McGee2022bStone2022Thomas2022b_239-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWalkerElgotAllegretti2022McGee2022bStone2022Thomas2022b-239">&#91;234&#93;</a></sup> and a chaotic <a href="/wiki/Premiership_of_Liz_Truss#Resignations_and_vote_on_fracking" title="Premiership of Liz Truss">vote on fracking</a> along with the resignation of Braverman as home secretary compounded a rapid deterioration of confidence in her leadership.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEWoodcock2022aWalkerGrierson2022&quot;MPs_allege_bullying_during_chaotic_fracking_vote&quot;._BBC_NewsBrown2022WhannelMason2022_240-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWoodcock2022aWalkerGrierson2022&quot;MPs_allege_bullying_during_chaotic_fracking_vote&quot;._BBC_NewsBrown2022WhannelMason2022-240">&#91;235&#93;</a></sup> On 19 October, in response to a <a href="/wiki/Prime_Minister%27s_Questions" title="Prime Minister&#39;s Questions">question</a> by the leader of the opposition, <a href="/wiki/Keir_Starmer" title="Keir Starmer">Keir Starmer</a>, Truss said that she was a "fighter and not a quitter", quoting a 2001 phrase by <a href="/wiki/Peter_Mandelson" title="Peter Mandelson">Peter Mandelson</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEQuinn2022b_241-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEQuinn2022b-241">&#91;236&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Shortly before noon on 20 October, Truss's 45th day in office, Brady held a meeting with Truss where she asked if she would be able to remain in office;<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEBogdanor2022570_231-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBogdanor2022570-231">&#91;226&#93;</a></sup> his response was "I don't think so, prime minister".<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEColeHeale2022309_242-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEColeHeale2022309-242">&#91;237&#93;</a></sup> At 1:35 pm, Truss announced her resignation as the leader of the Conservative Party and as prime minister. She gave the following 89-second-long statement:
</p>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r996844942"><blockquote class="templatequote"><p>I came into office at a time of great economic and international instability. Families and businesses were worried about how to pay their bills. Putin's illegal war in Ukraine threatens the security of our whole continent. And our country has been held back by for too long by low economic growth. I was elected by the Conservative Party with a mandate to change this. We delivered on energy bills and on cutting National Insurance. And we set out a vision for a low-tax, high-growth economy that would take advantage of the freedoms of Brexit. I recognise though, given the situation, I cannot deliver the mandate on which I was elected by the Conservative Party. I have therefore spoken to His Majesty the King to notify him that I am resigning as leader of the Conservative Party. This morning I met the chairman of the 1922 Committee, Sir <a href="/wiki/Graham_Brady" title="Graham Brady">Graham Brady</a>. We've agreed there will be a leadership election to be completed within the next week. This will ensure that we remain on a path to deliver our fiscal plans and maintain our country's economic stability and national security. I will remain as prime minister until a successor has been chosen. Thank you.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEColeHeale2022310_243-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEColeHeale2022310-243">&#91;238&#93;</a></sup></p></blockquote>
<p>She was <a href="/wiki/October_2022_Conservative_Party_leadership_election" title="October 2022 Conservative Party leadership election">succeeded</a> by Sunak as leader of the Conservative Party on 24 October and, the next day, advised the King to appoint him as the new prime minister;<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEAllegretti2022_244-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEAllegretti2022-244">&#91;239&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMorris2022a_245-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMorris2022a-245">&#91;240&#93;</a></sup> Sunak went on to further reverse many of the economic measures she had made as prime minister but retained Hunt as chancellor.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHicksonWilliams20238_218-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHicksonWilliams20238-218">&#91;213&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDiamondRichardsSandersWestwood20237HicksonWilliams20238_246-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDiamondRichardsSandersWestwood20237HicksonWilliams20238-246">&#91;241&#93;</a></sup> Truss became the <a href="/wiki/List_of_prime_ministers_of_the_United_Kingdom_by_length_of_tenure" title="List of prime ministers of the United Kingdom by length of tenure">shortest-serving prime minister</a> in British history, surpassing <a href="/wiki/George_Canning" title="George Canning">George Canning</a>, who was prime minister for 119 days in 1827.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHicksonWilliams20233ColeHeale2022310_247-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHicksonWilliams20233ColeHeale2022310-247">&#91;242&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-248" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-248">&#91;f&#93;</a></sup> The short length of her premiership was the subject of much ridicule, including <a href="/wiki/Liz_Truss_lettuce" title="Liz Truss lettuce">a livestream of a head of lettuce</a>, started the week prior, which invited viewers to speculate whether Truss would resign before the lettuce wilted.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTE&quot;Newspaper_headlines:_&#39;Time_up&#39;_as_&#39;Truss_clings_on_by_sacking_Kwarteng&#39;&quot;._BBC_News_249-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTE&quot;Newspaper_headlines:_&#39;Time_up&#39;_as_&#39;Truss_clings_on_by_sacking_Kwarteng&#39;&quot;._BBC_News-249">&#91;243&#93;</a></sup> Truss, who remains in the Commons as a backbencher, submitted in 2023 the list of her <a href="/wiki/Prime_Minister%27s_Resignation_Honours" title="Prime Minister&#39;s Resignation Honours">resignation honours</a> which as of September are yet to be approved.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDathan2023_250-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDathan2023-250">&#91;244&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Political_positions">Political positions</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Liz_Truss&amp;action=edit&amp;section=17" title="Edit section: Political positions">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Domestic_issues">Domestic issues</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Liz_Truss&amp;action=edit&amp;section=18" title="Edit section: Domestic issues">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Truss is known for her <a href="/wiki/Economic_liberalism" title="Economic liberalism">economically liberal</a> views and support for <a href="/wiki/Free_trade" title="Free trade">free trade</a> and <a href="/wiki/Deregulation" title="Deregulation">deregulation</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDiamondRichardsSandersWestwood20232_251-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDiamondRichardsSandersWestwood20232-251">&#91;245&#93;</a></sup> She supports the <a href="/wiki/Neoliberalism" title="Neoliberalism">neoliberal</a> philosophy of <a href="/wiki/Supply-side_economics" title="Supply-side economics">supply-side economics</a>, often referred to as "<a href="/wiki/Trickle-down_economics" title="Trickle-down economics">trickle-down economics</a>".<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEElliott2022_252-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEElliott2022-252">&#91;246&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEGilchrist2022_253-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEGilchrist2022-253">&#91;247&#93;</a></sup> In 2021 Truss stated that the Conservatives should "reject the <a href="/wiki/Zero-sum_game" title="Zero-sum game">zero-sum game</a> of <a href="/wiki/Identity_politics" title="Identity politics">identity politics</a>, [reject] the illiberalism of <a href="/wiki/Cancel_culture" title="Cancel culture">cancel culture</a>, and [reject] the <a href="/wiki/Soft_bigotry_of_low_expectations" class="mw-redirect" title="Soft bigotry of low expectations">soft bigotry of low expectations</a> that holds so many people back".<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMasonElgotAllegretti2021_254-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMasonElgotAllegretti2021-254">&#91;248&#93;</a></sup> She voted for <a href="/wiki/Same-sex_marriage_in_the_United_Kingdom" title="Same-sex marriage in the United Kingdom">gay marriage</a> but has opposed the expansion of <a href="/wiki/Transgender_rights_in_the_United_Kingdom" title="Transgender rights in the United Kingdom">transgender rights</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMcAuley2022_255-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMcAuley2022-255">&#91;249&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEPerry2023_256-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEPerry2023-256">&#91;250&#93;</a></sup> Truss spoke against <a href="/wiki/Gender_self-identification" title="Gender self-identification">gender self-identification</a>, stating that "medical checks are important" and that "only women have a cervix".<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDiver2021_257-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDiver2021-257">&#91;251&#93;</a></sup> Despite initially supporting single-sex toilets being restricted on the basis of biological sex, she later said in February 2022 that the government was not interested in enacting such a measure.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEChaplain2022_258-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEChaplain2022-258">&#91;252&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Foreign_policy">Foreign policy</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Liz_Truss&amp;action=edit&amp;section=19" title="Edit section: Foreign policy">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Two_protesters_outside_Downing_Street_highlighting_the_UK%27s_complicity_in_Saudi_Arabia%27s_bombing_campaign_against_Yemen.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img alt="Two women at the Downing Street gates holding placards reading &quot;British bombs are killing Yemeni children&quot; and &quot;Bombing a schoolbus is OK by Liz Truss #stopkillingyemenichildren&quot;." src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/70/Two_protesters_outside_Downing_Street_highlighting_the_UK%27s_complicity_in_Saudi_Arabia%27s_bombing_campaign_against_Yemen.jpg/220px-Two_protesters_outside_Downing_Street_highlighting_the_UK%27s_complicity_in_Saudi_Arabia%27s_bombing_campaign_against_Yemen.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="124" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/70/Two_protesters_outside_Downing_Street_highlighting_the_UK%27s_complicity_in_Saudi_Arabia%27s_bombing_campaign_against_Yemen.jpg/330px-Two_protesters_outside_Downing_Street_highlighting_the_UK%27s_complicity_in_Saudi_Arabia%27s_bombing_campaign_against_Yemen.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/70/Two_protesters_outside_Downing_Street_highlighting_the_UK%27s_complicity_in_Saudi_Arabia%27s_bombing_campaign_against_Yemen.jpg/440px-Two_protesters_outside_Downing_Street_highlighting_the_UK%27s_complicity_in_Saudi_Arabia%27s_bombing_campaign_against_Yemen.jpg 2x" data-file-width="8142" data-file-height="4580" /></a><figcaption>Protesters highlighting the UK's complicity in Saudi Arabia's <a href="/wiki/Saudi-led_intervention_in_the_Yemeni_civil_war" title="Saudi-led intervention in the Yemeni civil war">bombing campaign against Yemen</a></figcaption></figure>
<p>Truss was described as a <a href="/wiki/War_hawk" title="War hawk">hawkish</a> foreign secretary.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTELandler2022Adler2022Bray2022Mitter2022_259-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTELandler2022Adler2022Bray2022Mitter2022-259">&#91;253&#93;</a></sup> She called for the UK to reduce its <a href="/wiki/China%E2%80%93United_Kingdom_relations" title="China–United Kingdom relations">economic dependency on China</a> and <a href="/wiki/Russia%E2%80%93United_Kingdom_relations" title="Russia–United Kingdom relations">Russia</a> and supported certain diplomatic and economic sanctions imposed by the British government against the former.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECourea2022_260-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECourea2022-260">&#91;254&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTENi2022_261-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTENi2022-261">&#91;255&#93;</a></sup> Truss has supported <a href="/wiki/Taiwan" title="Taiwan">Taiwan</a> in the context of deteriorating <a href="/wiki/Cross-Strait_relations" title="Cross-Strait relations">cross-strait relations</a> but, citing precedent, said that she would not visit the island as prime minister<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHolly2022_262-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHolly2022-262">&#91;256&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEThykjaerLandauro2022_263-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEThykjaerLandauro2022-263">&#91;257&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEJamesMacaskill2022_264-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEJamesMacaskill2022-264">&#91;258&#93;</a></sup> and condemned the Chinese government's <a href="/wiki/Uyghur_genocide" title="Uyghur genocide">treatment of the Uyghur people</a> as "genocide".<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEWintour2020_265-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWintour2020-265">&#91;259&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDathan2021_266-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDathan2021-266">&#91;260&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEColeHeale2022222_267-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEColeHeale2022222-267">&#91;261&#93;</a></sup> In 2022 she called Saudi Arabia an ally but said she was not "condoning" the country's policies, including its <a href="/wiki/Human_rights_in_Saudi_Arabia" title="Human rights in Saudi Arabia">handling of human rights</a> and its <a href="/wiki/Women%27s_rights_in_Saudi_Arabia" title="Women&#39;s rights in Saudi Arabia">treatment of women</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEPlummer2022_268-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEPlummer2022-268">&#91;262&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Truss supported the United Kingdom remaining in the European Union during the 2016 referendum.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEColeHeale2022120–124_269-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEColeHeale2022120–124-269">&#91;263&#93;</a></sup> Since the referendum, Truss has supported Brexit; in 2017 she publicly stated that she had changed her mind.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMathers2021_270-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMathers2021-270">&#91;264&#93;</a></sup> During the July 2022 leadership election, Truss said that "I was wrong and I am prepared to admit I was wrong".<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEPickard2022_271-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEPickard2022-271">&#91;265&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Notes_and_references">Notes and references</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Liz_Truss&amp;action=edit&amp;section=20" title="Edit section: Notes and references">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p><b>Notes</b>
</p>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1011085734">.mw-parser-output .reflist{font-size:90%;margin-bottom:0.5em;list-style-type:decimal}.mw-parser-output .reflist .references{font-size:100%;margin-bottom:0;list-style-type:inherit}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns-2{column-width:30em}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns-3{column-width:25em}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns{margin-top:0.3em}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns ol{margin-top:0}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns li{page-break-inside:avoid;break-inside:avoid-column}.mw-parser-output .reflist-upper-alpha{list-style-type:upper-alpha}.mw-parser-output .reflist-upper-roman{list-style-type:upper-roman}.mw-parser-output .reflist-lower-alpha{list-style-type:lower-alpha}.mw-parser-output .reflist-lower-greek{list-style-type:lower-greek}.mw-parser-output .reflist-lower-roman{list-style-type:lower-roman}</style><div class="reflist reflist-lower-alpha">
<div class="mw-references-wrap"><ol class="references">
<li id="cite_note-1"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-1">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">As Minister for Equalities.</span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-6"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-6">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">John and Priscilla had three children after Truss: Chris (born 1978), Patrick (born 1980) and Francis (born 1983).<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEGlancyDaniel2022_3-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEGlancyDaniel2022-3">&#91;2&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEGryllsNorfolkWace2022_4-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEGryllsNorfolkWace2022-4">&#91;3&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEColeHeale20228_5-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEColeHeale20228-5">&#91;4&#93;</a></sup></span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-117"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-117">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">Excluding <a href="/wiki/Eleanor_of_Provence" title="Eleanor of Provence">Eleanor of Provence</a>, who exercised the powers of the lord chancellor in 1253 but was not formally appointed to the office.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEColeHeale2022132_115-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEColeHeale2022132-115">&#91;113&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEBowcott2016_116-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBowcott2016-116">&#91;114&#93;</a></sup></span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-141"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-141">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">Williamson had also requested a five per cent rise in troops' pay.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEColeHeale2022169_140-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEColeHeale2022169-140">&#91;137&#93;</a></sup></span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-171"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-171">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">Truss succeeded in March 2022 and called the release of Zaghari-Ratcliffe and <a href="/wiki/Anoosheh_Ashoori" title="Anoosheh Ashoori">Anoosheh Ashoori</a> the "most privileged moment" of her time in government.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEColeHeale2022232_170-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEColeHeale2022232-170">&#91;166&#93;</a></sup></span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-248"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-248">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">Canning's premiership ended with his death, rather than the loss of a general election or by resignation.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEColeHeale2022310_243-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEColeHeale2022310-243">&#91;238&#93;</a></sup></span>
</li>
</ol></div></div>
